tbmm-2021-07-13sit04spe0270par0216	Mr. President, I would like to present our views on behalf of the Good Party Group on the whole of the Law on Tourism Incentivisation Act and the Law Proposal on Change in Some Laws. Sadly, the laws that have been passed so far have caused various challenges because they are not clear, understandable, and the results are not predictable. Therefore, this method must be abandoned at once, both in the simplicity of our legislation and in the possible sense of running democratic negotiations. Members of lawmakers, the current government has long imposed a series of policies against our forests, our meres, our seas, our lakes, our subterranean waters and aboveground waters. In Turkey, there seems to be no new news that threatens nature and the environment, destroying forests. In Article 169 of the Constitution, no activity or action that can harm the forest is permitted. The most important reason for the threat to Turkey's forest existence is that the government encourages it. This proposal is an arrangement that threatens the presence of the forest in Turkey and creates new risks. Under the first article of the proposal, the president is granted the authority to declare the places he wants to be a cultural and tourism protection and development zone, and the state's ruling and saving forest areas are declared a tourist zone. So there is also a new threat of construction for forestry areas, as well as authority and responsibilities for local governments are being transferred to central administration. Again, we are concerned that the fourth article of the proposal, along with the coastal areas, will also lead to tourism construction. Similarly, there is a risk that the 6th article, the meres, the plateaus, and the forestry areas will be opened to construction. Again, the 17th and 21st articles of the proposal also make arrangements for the opening of forest areas. All of these regulations show us that it aims to plunder forests under the development of power tourism and to destroy more of our forests, which are the lungs of Turkey. The risks of wanting forest areas to open up are not just trees to be cut down for structures, but the construction will increase the risks of destroying ecosystems and forest fires by taking more of the settlements into the forest areas. As our country and our world face a great disaster like the climate crisis more and more, it is impossible to understand why the steps are being taken to speed up this crisis and what its purpose is. Therefore, it is not possible for us to support such an offer because it does not have any analysis of the impact and environmental impact assessment of forests, shores, and meras. Because we, the PTA Party Group, are considering the proposal as a very dangerous and risky attempt at our country's forest existence and animalry activities. Honourable lawmakers have yet to remove the trauma of the 2019 local elections. In 2019, the National Alliance of Nations is trying to stave off its achievements in many constituencies by transferring the powers of its local governments to central administration. In every area in the past two years, the government has done its best to weaken local governments, and has shown once again today that it is continuing the same wrong policy as this offer. License permits for the opening and running of the proposal are still under the obligation of municipalities to obtain a Tourism Management Document for housing businesses. The permit to open and run a tourist business from municipalities is also revoked. We also need to state that the exclusion of public guesthouses is an unjust competition when the third article is conditioned on documenting all accommodation and beach businesses. A similar situation in the scythe of the authority of local governments is also seen as opening up luxury tent spaces under Article 4. In terms of luxury camps, local governments have been pushed out of the process. In the same way, municipalities are isolated from the development processes of tourism areas in the protected areas. Similarly, local governments are excluded and the Ministry of Culture and Tourism is authorized to plan in tourist protection areas. In this way, with the approval of the Ministry of Environment and Protection Regional Commission, investment demands can be finalised within six months. We think these processes should be transparent and accountable, as we care to quickly conclude investors' demands. In Article 17 of the proposal, the Ministry of Culture and Tourism has the authority to allocate fixed values in forest areas and places of employment in coastal towns. In this regard, we think that the areas outside the tourism area and the centers of the mesir area should be under the authority of the municipalities of the district. In this way, the proposal is in violation of the Constitution in many ways of Article 127, which regulates the powers and responsibilities of local governments. In short, this proposal is like a crystallized state of power's policy on neutralizing local governments. At this point, as the Good Party, we would like to say once again that a political understanding that excludes local governments from public administration is not a useful concept for our country and democracy. This idea of politics is an understanding that transfers the powers of the chosen to the appointed, exploiting administrative custody over local governments. While honoring lawmakers discuss such a law proposal that concerns the tourism sector closely, we should also consider what tourism means to Turkey's economy. As you know, the tourism industry is our sector that has the least dependency on imports and has the highest proportional value. It's technically the only sector where resources are unlimited and sustainable when used correctly. The importance of Turkey's economy in terms of the current deficit and the balance of payments is undisputed. Tourism is the only area of our competitiveness in the international arena. About 1,250,000 people are employed in the tourism industry. If we take into account the shareholders and the sectors that tourism has indirectly influenced, millions of our citizens have been able to make a living on tourism revenues, but as we all know, this sector has been affected in a variety of cases -- except for the 2015 plane crisis until now -- if we keep it out of the 2019 years. Our tourism sector was practically intubated in 2020 with the Covid-19 infection that emerged in China and spread throughout the world. Because of the Covid-19 effect, the tourism movement in the world, as in our country, has stopped for a long time, and the global tourism market has been significantly smaller. Therefore, the United Nations World Tourism Organization stated that 2020 was the worst year of global tourism in history. In 2020, there was a loss of $1 trillion of $300 billion in the world's tourism market. According to data from the Ministry of Culture and Tourism, in 2019 our country received 51,750,000 tourists, while about 16 million tourists visited in 2020, so according to the previous year, the number of tourists was reduced by 35,750,000 and the number of tourists declined by 69 per cent. In 2019, the tourism sector earned $34.5 billion in revenues, while in 2020, about 65 per cent lost, making about $12 billion in revenues. According to the joint report of the United Nations World Tourism Organisation and the United Nations Conference on Trade and Development, Turkey became the country that lost the most tourism revenues compared to national income in 2020, and the loss of its Turkish lira in an extraordinary way could not prevent it. So it is clear that Turkey is the country that has lost the most tourism revenues in the world compared to national income, and the government's extraordinary mismanagement of the pandemic is the country that has lost the most tourism revenues in the world. Members of parliament, the tourism sector, which has already faced various challenges in recent years, has become a life-and-death battle with the planless, unscheduled, aimless and unscheduled management of the pandemic. The sector's debt at 76 billion liras two years ago has risen to $130 billion in two years, so the debt of the sector is almost doubled in two years because of the wrong pandemic management of power that chooses to give loans to businesses instead of giving them direct grants and support, as in the world. The power, which did not give the tourist any real support, continued to receive dues for Turkey's Tourism Promotion and Development Agency. This offer, brought to the General Assembly by members of the ruling party, brings new burdens to the sector, with punishments he has organised, as well as no incentive or support for the tourism sector. To give an example: Those who fail to receive the Security Tourism Certificate in fifteen days after the Ministry's warning, and those who fail to submit the document on the payment of the Tourism Promotion and Development Agency will be fined $11,500. This amount is a large sum for a large number of small businesses in the tourism sector and needs to be reviewed. Honourable lawmakers have failed to support tourism workers in the pandemic process and have allowed them to be devastated. Tourism workers, however, are a very important and highly qualified class that sustains the tourism industry. The government, which has done nothing against the problems of our own tourism workers, has made an arrangement to increase the employment of foreign workers in the tourism sector. Under Article 8 of the proposal, the limit of working foreign workers in tourism businesses is increased from 10 to 20 per cent, so there is a 100 per cent increase. Is it right or is it appropriate to increase the limits of working foreign workers in a country called "house youth" where the large number of unemployed exceeds 10 million, and one of the four college graduates can't find a job, while millions of young people are now called "house youth"? Our recommendation to power in this regard is that instead of opening the labour market that has already been turned upside down by refugees to more foreigners, it is to implement policies that will integrate our unemployed young people into tourism and many vital sectors. Honourable lawmakers, our tourism potential for Turkey to be a richer and more prosperous country is significantly above the performance that has been performed so far. For this potential to be fulfilled, the current government has not revealed any serious arrangement, plan, program and strategy. Because power's horizons, vision, and problem-solving abilities don't allow it. So for years, the government has been installed in construction to revive the economy, and in tourism, it tries to distance itself from its concreteisation policies. The power's desperation for tourism and its inefficient policies are reflected in numbers. 14.5 per cent of Turkey's existing hotels are five stars. In Italy and France, which are our competitors and have more tourism revenues than we do, the proportion is less than 5 per cent. The same is true in Spain, which is also an important tourist destinization and our rival. The number of five-star hotels in Turkey has been increasing, while tourism per capita spending has decreased over the past 20 years, and even this data is sufficient evidence to show the inefficientness of the concrete economy of power for tourism. So our tourism industry is in a blockage and a mediocrity trap in terms of efficiency. To increase the competitiveness and efficiency of the tourism industry, we need a radical change of vision and radical reforms in our tourism policies. In this regard, we need to build an integrated tourism and logistical infrastructure, boosting our traditional culture's tourist promotion, and most importantly, a series of radical structural transformations, which are believed to have an independent judiciary that values human rights and freedoms in the outside world, which is believed to have value, an independent judiciary, a democratic, peaceful and stable image of a country based on education. In the coming period, however, tourism trends in the world are expected to change. In this direction, there have been serious expectations, such as the decline of mass tourism in the future, increased individual travel, increased competition in alternative tourism, reduced travel periods in tourism, but increased spending, preferring places based on environment, nature, culture, ecoturism, adventure tourism, and the growing share of rural tourism. It is unlikely that Turkey will respond to these trends with its current tourism policy and with its ruling mentality because we must first correct our position in environmental sustainability indicators. Because, as you can see in this proposal, to try to gain distance by destroying the environment to improve tourism is to bring the risk of eliminating tourism completely in the future. As the HRP, we aim to go to diversity in tourism with an understanding that extends time and space, to create sustainable policies that bring people and the world to its center, respect for the environment, history, culture, to achieve digital transformations in tourism, transforming global developments into opportunity. As I stop speaking with these feelings and thoughts, I salute your grand delegation.

As a country, increasing our share of the world's tourism cake is one of the main goals set by the Ministry of Culture and Tourism, becoming one of the world's most visited countries with most tourist revenues. One of the most affected sectors of the Covid-19 epidemic, which began to dominate the world by the end of 2019, became the tourism industry. Our Ministry of Culture and Tourism has launched the Safe Tourism Certification Programme, one of the first examples in the world, to minimize the negative effects of the Covid-19 epidemic on the sector, and to ensure that the tourism movements that have begun gradually move towards our country. Thanks to the successful execution of this program, our losses in the number of visitors to our country during the pandemic period were about 60 per cent, while our competitors suffered between 75 per cent and 80 per cent of the tape. The number of visitors to our country in 2020 reached 16 million, despite Covid-19 pandemics.

One of the elements necessary for sustainable tourism to continue uninterrupted is the introduction of a certain standard in the tourism sector. The law we are meeting today envisions some significant changes to ensure these standards. For example, tourism facilities are required to obtain a Tourism Management Document from the Ministry to ensure that the Ministry of Culture and Tourism has control and control of them. Similarly, the management of marine tourism facilities such as croissant ports, marinas, pulleys, piers, and piers is required by the Ministry of Culture and Tourism to obtain Tourism Management Documents. If this document is not received, a fine is required. These two examples we've given are a clear indication of how sensitive the Ministry is about setting standards.
